The TLS repair is test-only: mint a valid disconnect session, use keyed notification settlement, retain zero unexpected desynchronisation and at-least-one intentional rejection, and stop a failed fixture owner through shutdown cleanup.

Fresh security traces and conclusions:
1. Durable rotation ownership: api_token_store.cpp around 1620–1685 keeps epoch validation before the new durable initiator mismatch denial, and denial before replay-cache reservation. A durable initiator string grants no raw-secret authority. Matching identity still needs the RAM replay entry; restart/other replica/cache loss stays conflict. Existing malformed/overfull linked-state checks and confirm/revoke/sweep transitions retain prior reviewed behavior. No secret publication precedes successful transaction completion.
2. Current PostgreSQL with_txn guard (pg_pool.cpp around 389–433): false callback rolls back before releasing the lease; true requires an in-transaction connection before commit. New denial follows that failure path and existing scrub guards. No new handle ownership escape or manual cleanup waiver.
3. Current REST rotate caller (rest_api_v1.cpp around 7050–7225) retains permission/store/session gates, engine denial, fresh step-up, bounds, and current session attribution. Checked successor read withholds the raw secret on read failure or missing pending link. Reveal audit must persist before disclosure. Current MCP rotate (mcp_server.cpp around 29805–30000) preserves tier-before-permission, engine denial, bounded integer input, session attribution and checked read; failed reveal audit withholds the secret. Current classifier still treats ownership/epoch conflicts as non-retry conflict. No new trust or audit bypass arises from upstream route changes.
4. grpc worker patch freshly reread: unique_ptr<ThreadState> is declared before scoped noncopyable OpenSSL cleanup; reverse destruction calls OPENSSL_thread_stop before worker retirement on normal return and ordinary C++ unwind. Secure-grpc private definition and supported OpenSSL guard exclude BoringSSL/LibreSSL/old OpenSSL. This is current-thread cleanup, not global crypto shutdown or an OS-thread join. Existing callback queue/drain boundaries remain the exact claim.
5. Runner lifetime: default-engine reference transferred into suite-owned slot; Catch session ends before blocking grpc shutdown and default-engine shutdown. Agent runner excludes this owner for fork quiescence. Sanitizer/coverage guards return normally; command-line error paths unwind. No claim that unrelated caller-owned engines/threads are joined. Prior full-file and dependency implementation reviews are reused by byte proof.
6. Current build_examples shell fixture read fully: Meson-owned argv is copied into a quoted Bash array, without eval. Unique mktemp directory is the cleanup owner; trap removes only that directory. Config failures expose logs and exit. Upstream native-host no-server assertion and authored parent dependency/sanitizer arguments coexist.
7. Resolved Docker agent context keeps COPY ports before dependency installation and upstream explicit server=disabled. Parent overlay license is included. Meson native agent profile still registers the shared grpc runtime fixture without server/libpq dependency. Upstream feature migration changes validation scope, not the rotation security model.

UP-1 Commit succeeds, response lost, original operator retries before RAM publication: conflict rather than second mint; deterministic hook exercises exact gap. Existing regression/root PG execution, no additional chaos needed.
UP-2 Different operator races during that gap: durable initiator mismatch denies before reservation; no secret or cache occupancy granted. Existing deterministic regression.
UP-3 Same operator retries on another replica or after restart: durable ownership does not confer reveal authority; absent RAM entry conflicts. Inspect/revoke recovery, not automatic repeated mint. Existing cases/root execution.
UP-4 Retry after epoch expires: epoch error retains precedence over ownership; no stale replay. Existing precedence assertion.
UP-5 Manual successor revoke/confirm between commit and read-back: checked read finds no pending link, withholds raw secret and avoids automatic retry hint. Current REST/MCP partial outcome remains visible. Existing boundary tests; do not weaken to best-effort read.
UP-6 Read-back DB fault or reveal audit failure: no secret leaves either transport; partial/error response may need same-operator/replica bounded recovery. Error classification prevents a permanent owner conflict becoming a retry storm. Existing route/store tests.
UP-7 Malformed/overfull/zero-row durable state during rotate: existing guards reject rather than guess a pair. New compare operates only after state admission. Existing state-machine tests; no new malformed-state repair promised.
UP-8 Worker completes crypto callback while suite teardown starts: local cleanup runs before retirement counter; default-engine owner drains after Catch session. Timeout callback holds shared promise, not borrowed stack. Root normal-exit repeats/instrumented deps needed; no OS-thread-join claim.
UP-9 Assertion or CLI parse error unwinds early: runner and fixture RAII still releases owners; agent fork harness stays separate. Guardian Rig releases parked gates before engine wait. Existing failure-path tests/repeats, not sleep-based proof.
UP-10 Partial/cancelled dependency install: coherent cache save gated on install success; overlay content changes invalidate sentinel. Native vcpkg profile migration and historical half-installed purge limits remain documented. Current sentinel/profile validation pending.

Server-dependent healthcheck remains build_server-gated. Windows current profile execution pending; no whole macOS proof inferred.
UP-13 Gateway fixture crypto policy rejects SHA1 or EdDSA is mishandled: RSA/EC now explicitly SHA256, intrinsic EdDSA untouched; current nonzero EUnit/CT and Dialyzer must run. No production acceptance policy relaxed.
UP-14 Python delayed handler unwind/EOF during cleanup: logical request completion precedes observed response, cleanup idempotent; MCP stdin detached after EOF before communicate, timeout retains kill/drain. Existing regression tests cover reset/delay rather than relying on scheduler luck.

Resource | Owner / acquisition | Release / transfer / failure cleanup
1. Default EventEngine and pool: SuiteGrpcRuntime lifecycle owner; CreateEventEngine returns shared_ptr, moved by SetDefaultEventEngine into strong global default slot before grpc_init. No extra runner shared_ptr retained. Destructor calls grpc_shutdown_blocking then ShutdownDefaultEventEngine, whose inspected implementation removes slot and waits for single ownership. Catch Session is declared later and destroyed first on normal return, command-line error, or C++ unwind after completed construction. Create failure has ordinary temporary shared_ptr cleanup. Agent runner does not compile this owner. Installed-version behavior and any constructor partial-publication failure remain Gate 3 verification obligations.
2. gRPC C initialization reference: SuiteGrpcRuntime obtains one grpc_init reference after engine installation, balances with grpc_shutdown_blocking in destructor, noncopyable. No explicit manual cleanup outside owner. Other suite init refs, custom engines, and caller threads remain their individual fixtures' responsibility; this scope does not imply ownership of them.
3. Catch Session and listener resources: run_suite stack Session, applyCommandLine/session.run; destructor before SuiteGrpcRuntime, including early CLI failure. Existing PG testRunEnded cleanup executes during session.run; main hard_exit is now after run_suite destruction. Sanitizer/coverage paths return normally. stdout/stderr are borrowed process streams, only fprintf/fflush, not acquired/closed here; argc/argv borrowed for call duration.
4. Detached upstream EventEngine workers: existing StartThread allocates new ThreadState(shared_from_this()), transfers void* context to detached grpc_core::Thread callback, and callback immediately adopts it in unique_ptr<ThreadState>. ThreadState holds the pool shared_ptr and living-thread counter. The callback-local owner releases on normal return or ordinary C++ unwind. Upstream thread creation failure/termination behavior is unchanged. No native OS-thread join is introduced; quiescence counts retirement.
5. Worker implicit OpenSSL thread state: crypto callbacks acquire it on that worker. Noncopyable ScopedOpenSslThreadState is declared after the unique_ptr and its destructor calls OPENSSL_thread_stop before worker destruction/counter retirement, on normal return and ordinary unwind. No transfer or global context ownership. Guard applies only secure grpc with supported OpenSSL >=1.1, excluding BoringSSL/LibreSSL. New cleanup is RAII-wrapped; no manual-cleanup exception proposed or self-adjudicated.
6. Upstream local work queue / callback queue / thread-local bookkeeping: ThreadBody new BasicWorkQueue(pool_.get()), enrolls queue, borrows pool through ThreadState's shared_ptr. Fork path moves pending closures into pool queue; shutdown FinishDraining runs them; unenroll then delete queue before new OpenSSL stop. Existing raw callback contexts and bookkeeping retain upstream ownership. Patch cannot attest that arbitrary future thread-local destructors never reenter crypto; its exact boundary is callbacks drained and this thread's current OpenSSL state stopped before retirement.
7. Lifetime regression's EventEngine refs: temporary shared_ptr from GetDefaultEventEngine assigned to weak observed; temporary destroyed before expired assertion. observed.lock gives local shared owner through scheduling and future wait. All local refs destroyed on Catch REQUIRE unwinding as on success; timeout does not cancel queued work but suite-owned engine remains until runner teardown.
8. Lifetime regression promise/future/callback: make_shared<promise<bool>>, one future via get_future; lambda captures shared_ptr by value, EventEngine owns submitted callback until invocation/disposal. Main and lambda release their copies independently; future destroys its shared-state ref. Worker uses stack array<unsigned char,32> passed to RAND_bytes/RAND_priv_bytes only for call duration, no escape. Main-thread RAND_status and worker public/private DRBG allocate implicit OpenSSL thread state. Main state is process/main-thread lifecycle; worker state uses patched cleanup. Callback set_value is single-use; no borrowed stack capture. Timeout REQUIRE unwinds locals safely without invalidating callback's promise.
9. Rotation test PostgreSQL database: YUZU_REQUIRE_PG_DB_TPL creates stack PostgresTestDb from existing apitoken_pg_template. Missing DSN SKIPs before acquisition; unavailable database REQUIRE unwinds fixture. Helper owns salted database name/DSN; scoped PgConn/PgResult wrap admin handles/results. Destructor issues DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS WITH(FORCE), logging failure; this test does not call keep_until_run_end. Template itself uses pre-existing suite/template cleanup lifecycle.
10. Rotation test PgPool and ApiTokenStore: pool declared after DB, store after pool; reverse destruction destroys store, drains pool, then drops DB. PgPool size four owns pooled PGconn (network/TLS resources); destructor blocks until leases/connectors/waiters return and PQfinish closes idle handles. Store borrows pool reference. Test adds no persistent lease or transaction escape.
11. Rotation callback and borrowed test locals: store owns std::function test_hook_before_store_rotation_raw_, capture [&] borrows store, principal, now, interleaved. Called synchronously after transaction commit and before raw-cache publication; nested rejected rotations do not mint and do not recursively invoke hook. Borrowed locals live through invocations; closure destruction does not dereference them even though later-declared locals die before store. Stateless engine-referent callback has no borrowed resources. Durable initiator string used only for comparison; no new secret copy or cache reservation. Epoch check still precedes denial; try_reserve still follows it. ApiToken header only documents this existing field's broader denial use.

New hang_next_watch acquires logical gate; wait_entered_hang observes worker parked; release_hang unblocks, drain_until_idle/wait_compliant settle before final sync. KV SQLite owned by KvStore. AttachProbe shared_ptr is retained by installed hook (existing helper); callbacks retain fixture borrowing contract. Mutex/CV locks use lock_guard/unique_lock; timed waits release locks on exit. No new OS fd, HANDLE, SOCKET, FILE*, sqlite3_stmt, mapped library, subprocess, BCrypt handle or allocated C string directly introduced in changed C++ beyond wrapped/transitive resources enumerated above.

.github/workflows/ci.yml

Thus no authored addition/deletion was lost, duplicated or altered by the auto-merges/resolution. Fresh contextual review focused on build/dependency semantics below; routing/security agent owns production upstream caller/security interactions, not this narrow report.

Relevant upstream changes and interactions
1. meson.options now declares feature server=auto, keeps deprecated boolean build_server=true. meson.build116ff derives build_server from Linux TARGET plus feature state and old off switch, rejects enabled on non-Linux and enabled+deprecatedfalse conflict before dependency lookup. Linux default still builds server. Agent Dockerfiles MUST explicitly use -Dserver=disabled because their target is Linux; merely removing the old flag would enable server without its dependency profile. Current resolved files use disabled correctly. Deprecated options in persistent build dirs remain honored; this merge does not itself retire them.
2. scripts/setup.sh now chooses --x-no-default-features --x-feature=tests for native Windows/macOS; Linux and Linux-target cross files retain full profile. CI Windows/macOS, nightly Windows and CodeQL Windows align agent+tests profiles. Release native profiles omit tests and explicitly disable server. This is upstream ADR0035 step3, not a new authored dependency ABI change. vcpkg.json/config/triplets and complete grpc overlay bytes remain unchanged by this merge; upstream profile changes reconcile installed trees but do not remove base grpc/OpenSSL. Test profile includes Catch2.
3. tests/meson.build still builds standalone grpc_test_runner/runtime test outside if build_server; dependencies are Catch2/grpc/OpenSSL, no libpq. Therefore new native server auto-off does not erase the lifetime regression. Runtime remains agent+server suite labeled and preserves durations=yes and60second timeout. New upstream registrations (server-feature lint, port-retry classifier and other current tests) mean historical179-suite counts cannot certify merged-source execution.
4. build_examples gating retains authored setup_args propagation of parent cmake/pkgconfig/sanitizer choices, and upstream adds non-Linux no-yuzu-server assertion using uname. These distinct changes coexist; ephemeral configure remains server auto, not accidentally explicitly enabled. Meson current argument wiring remains authored-identical.
5. CI authored canary ports/** hash and dependency classifier explanation remain exact; upstream agent-profile migration is elsewhere. Sentinel deliberately does not key profile: vcpkg install reconciles package selection, while overlay content/name changes remain invalidation inputs. Current build/CI docs explain this, including interrupted Windows libpq removal recovery. No reason to add an install-profile key merely for this merge.
6. Current build guide retains overlay cleanup/refresh/rebuild explanation while updating server feature and dependency profile instructions. CI docs retain full overlay digest/interpreter fallback explanation and update platform profile/agent scope. Auth retry-denial paragraph remains exact at current rotation section, without losing upstream auth additions. New server option does not change raw replay authority.
7. Upstream release/docs now gate image publication on all four platform builds. This integration neither removes that gate nor publishes an image. Upstream dependency-update OIDC lookup note is separate from unchanged grpc/OpenSSL overlay. Focused workflow diffs/context were inspected for profile integration; not claiming a complete new review of every upstream release/REST/MCP/Guardian implementation.

Docker conflict and smallest concrete validation
Resolved agent builder sequence at35–41: COPY manifests; COPY ports/ ports/; upstream comment naming -Dserver=disabled; vcpkg install --x-no-default-features. Meson at54–60 keeps both in-tree dependency search paths, build_tests=false and server=disabled. Against d625 the ONLY authored Dockerfile.agent delta is COPY ports/ ports/; upstream option/comment changes both remain. Chisel sibling has the same overlay-before-install/disabled semantics and retains sharing=locked cache mount. All5 authored Docker recipes still copy ports before their vcpkg install. No changed runtime image or COPY-from-builder rule required.
Read .dockerignore: ports not excluded; *.md/docs/tests rules do not exclude .patch/.cmake/.json/.txt overlay/license files. vcpkg-configuration.json overlay-ports=["ports"] resolves under /src in agent recipes. Parent ports/LICENSE-vcpkg.txt is carried with COPYports, preserving imported notice. Agent dependency profile keeps base grpc/OpenSSL despite excluding server/tests. Docker COPY layer includes patch bytes before install, so edits invalidate install layer cache.
Minimal SOURCE integration check is precisely: resolved file has no conflict markers; copy once before first vcpkg install; config-selected overlay and required inherited/local patch/helper/license files included by context; both old comment and configure option migrated to disabled; root-target server remains off with matching no-default feature selection. Current read establishes these semantics, and exhaustive delta equality supports preservation.
Minimal additional execution evidence root may choose: existing tests/test_server_feature_option.py exercises actual feature-option configure behavior and lexical profile contracts; current targeted sentinel/classifier regressions cover unchanged authored cache logic. Validation boundary
Previous ea21 source hash/sanitizer179x2 and focused Windows/Clang records remain historical for their exact source. They are useful unchanged-dependency provenance but do not certify new merged implementation or new registered tests. Record current merged source identity and actually executed checks separately. Root/routing govern rerouting and final validation scope. No new finding, source change request or policy exception proposed by this read-only integration analysis.

===== BEGIN VERBATIM integration-context.txt =====
Integration context and validation obligations — d625 (read-only analysis)
255 upstream paths, +18507/-1721 lines, distinct from47 authored paths. Full path manifest/stat and build deltas inspected; this is a broad integration survey plus complete build/Meson/workflow deltas, NOT a claim every upstream C++ body/fullfile has been reviewed. Long git-log and route grep outputs truncated; no claim of complete reading for those outputs. Saved full upstream-log.txt and integration-build.diff remain available.

Intersection: .github/workflows/ci.yml; deploy/docker/Dockerfile.agent and .agent.chisel; docs/auth-architecture.md, docs/build-guide.md, docs/ci-architecture.md; tests/meson.build; tests/shell/test_build_examples_gating.sh.
Root resolution observed in current diff: Dockerfile.agent retains COPY ports/ ports/ immediately after manifest copy AND upstream -Dserver=disabled documentation/configure. No conflict markers in observed hunk; root owns git-stage/final no-unmerged verification.

Highest integration obligation: ADR0035 step3 feature migration. meson.options server(feature,auto) replaces direct boolean decision; build_server deprecated still honored as extra off switch. Target system is lowercased. enabled on nonLinux refuses before dependency lookup; enabled with stored build_server=false refuses. --wipe replays old options, so validation must introspect actual build_server/server result, not assume reconfigure clears it. Linux full configured server+agent tests must remain present. On Windows/macOS auto disables server; CI intentionally passes NO server switch in persistent directories so old branches remain reconfigurable. Agent release/Docker clean configures explicitly -Dserver=disabled.
Native Windows/macOS CI and nightly/CodeQL now install --x-no-default-features --x-feature=tests; release installs --x-no-default-features alone. Shared grpc runtime test must configure/link with only agent+Catch dependencies (no accidental libpq/xmlsec/drogon). Prior manually minimal runner harness is useful ownership evidence but does NOT verify this changed Meson/vcpkg profile. Inspect actual agent-only configure dependency graph and registered runtime test; classify any unexecuted full native project build honestly.
New source-level regression tests: test_server_feature_option.py checks real predependency outcomes/lexical mutation guards; test_release_agent_only.py includes PowerShell archive DLL refusal; docs-lint now requires pwsh/PyYAML for that behavioral check. Release publish jobs now wait for Windows/macOS packaging too; source review must preserve all needs edges and DLL exclusions (libpq/ecpg/pgtypes/drogon/trantor/jsoncpp/xmlsec). No publishing is required for validation.
Authored shell fixture now accepts parent Meson cmake_prefix_path/pkg_config_path and sanitizer flag; default standalone triplet fallback may skip only when unpopulated. Re-run build_examples=false gating smoke under ASan and TSan parent paths; confirm target graph still excludes examples and includes tests. Standalone host detection differs from target/cross configuration; do not claim a cross configure from native only.

Other upstream integration surfaces to cover in fresh builds/tests:
- Echo/dedup SQLite open/quarantine and legacy rename now use shared RAII quarantine; busy-vs-corrupt, links, interrupted adoption, cleanup/lifetime behavior changed. Agent's no-runtime-owner fork/death-test policy remains relevant.
- Windows JVM no-reparse open and installed-apps input parsing changed. Platform suite and native Windows portability have new upstream source beyond previous focused harness.
- Result-set readiness/incomplete-under-NOT and unverified frame bookkeeping changed: broad server fixtures, worker/response lifetime and failure publication matter with suite default engine lifetime. No api_token_store implementation/header upstream diff; callers REST/MCP changed other operations and need targeted current rotation/context verification.
- OIDC bounded resolver pool/background resource shutdown, plugin-init-failed label registry and health recompute, Guardian census decommission changes add current background/store fixture behavior. Full sanitizer campaign must use current source; old179 count not assumed.
- Integration gateway launcher now retries only proved lost-port races with own-child process identity and a monotonic clock; Linux-only hermetic test and real-wire integration must run nonzero counts. Its TLS/key errors must not be mistaken for retryable bind faults.
- DEX UI/OpenAPI and input-hardening/management-group owner gate are upstream contracts; no authored route grant widened. Current server fullsuite catches their integration, but this survey is not independent security review of all upstream PRs.

Validation plan (root executes): freeze merged source manifest+base/parent identities; prove overlay/triplet/manifest bytes before dependency reuse; canonical install/dryrun and current server/agent configure; compile full Linux ASan+UBSan andTSan target graphs; normal-exit TLS/runtime repeats; full configured suites with actual fresh counts and nonzero EUnit/CT; full Dialyzer; local sentinel/classifier and feature/release/build_examples gates; native focused+agentprofile checks with explicit platform limits. Capture sanitizer options/suppressions/no-retry evidence, source hashes/exact exclusions, sanitized member-hashed bundle. No priorbase count/result should be carried forward as current.
No new finding raised by routing survey. Gate1 and actual domain reviews still required before sourcePASS; no gates/tests run independently by routing.

Gate 8 startup-fix governance synthesis — source review completed, runtime closure pending

Every affected gate was rerun for the one-file lifecycle fix. Skills and four routed tables were verified current against origin/dev d6256469a; row-by-row routing is retained in routing-row-walk.json. Gate 5 is skipped according to its explicit condition, not recorded as an execution pass. Reviewers reused across successive roles are not counted as independent confirmations.

Findings below remain PROVISIONAL presentation clusters; each has one independent reporter. No unrelated file/line findings are merged. Known baseline startup race is still blocking until candidate validation and review close it. QA's direct unwind/drain coverage gap is separate from pending execution and is not dismissed merely because existing suites pass.

ID | derived/native | location | current status | defect
startup-lifecycle-tsan-001 | BLOCKING/HIGH | agents/core/src/agent.cpp:960 | open | TSan reports data race run guardian_ assignment vs stop bare read; original full suite184OK1FAIL and focused repeat both show P1i/P1iii process exit66. Agent.cpp pre-fix HEAD equals origin/dev byte-for-byte, so defect predates integration. HIGH derives via I5(a): reproducible agent-process failure on ordinary shutdown lifecycle request; no unsupported claim of production data corruption or privilege escalation. Candidate publication+bounded DEX cleanup fix under review; runtime closure pending.
startup-docs-001 | SHOULD/SHOULD-FIX | changelog.d/20261110-nightly-shutdown-rotation.fixed.md:1 | open | Operator-visible safe shutdown during initialization and bounded DEX cleanup are added by startupfix but existing PRfragment covers only gRPC/OpenSSL and credentialrotation. Required-doc item3; add explicit coverage. Existingfragmentpresence acknowledged; no new missingfilefloor asserted.
startup-docs-002 | NICE/NICE-TO-HAVE | docs/yuzu-guardian-design-v1.1.md:2681 | open | After latchrename, Guardian§24 and spark_detached_call.hpp:44/test_spark_detached_call.cpp:768 retain old spark_boot_done_ name. Update references; detachedworker accounting invariant itself unchanged.
startup-security-001 | NICE/LOW | agents/core/src/agent.cpp:1441 | open | SparkAvailability::Unwired branch comment/log still attributes state to stop duringboot; new grouppublicationgate defers externalGuardianstop until afterwire/start_local, so statedtrigger no longerapplies. Keepdefensivearm butdescribe actualunwiredstate. No operationalcontrolweakening.
startup-qa-001 | NICE/SHOULD | agents/core/src/agent.cpp:989 | open | Q1: current tests do not directly observe DEX draining during unwind after start but before the later main cleanup, while AgentImpl remains alive, or serialized repeated stop. ===== security-guardian (verbatim) =====
Gate 2 security-guardian — startup publication fix
Reviewed: 2026-11-13T08:57:22.110812+00:00
Candidate agent.cpp SHA256: adb516a51671bfe8762de9247b5bc4b08b70b3c0689bb3bf4929b3998bf4069d
Diff SHA256: 67d319141e84db03974640cee411a79a7713844fe96e846e8697b965f5ac6f26
Distinctive source verified: if (lifecycle_boot_done_.load(std::memory_order_acquire)) {

Decision: no introduced security/ownership BLOCKING finding in the lifecycle fix. One NICE domain-truth observation below; candidate runtime closure remains PENDING. This is the Gate8 incremental re-review over the previously reviewed, byte-proven baseline, not a claim that prior runtime results apply to new source.

Context and scope: applied exact shared-preamble severity/policyfloor/prose contract and security-guardian brief. Read actual changed lifecycle regions and their run/stop callers, Guardian start_local/wire/stop ordering, DEX start/stop on Windows/Linux/macOS, main POSIX/Windows-console barriers and Windows SCM barrier, ShutdownDeadlineGuard, ScopeExit and member-order contracts. Relevant normative input: Guardian design §24 (worker joins, physical-worker F3 accounting, shutdown deadlines), accepted ADR0021 and Spark consumer lifecycle contracts, DEX callback ownership/privacy surface. No request/authz/crypto/parser/schema/wire changes. Existing TLS/cert handling and command dispatch outside the touched lifecycle remain prior-review scope; the diff neither adds credentials to logs nor new native/shell APIs.

Ownership and ordering proof:
1. run owns all slot mutation until release publication. External stop acquires the same atomic before any guardian_/dex_observer_/spark_engine_ read. The pointers remain immutable afterward until the existing watcher/SCM lifetime barrier precedes destruction. The false branch touches no unpublished pointer.
2. A pre-publication stop still sets the terminal request/cancellation flags. Its stop_completed_ latch does not suppress run-thread cleanup. Guardian and Spark retain their ungated cleanup and required Guardian-before-Spark order. Agent::stop is specified as signaling shutdown, not completing all run-thread startup synchronously; no component is re-started after its own sticky stop by this fix.
3. New DEX guard is armed before observer factory/start; factory failure leaves null, partial start/unwind sees same-thread owned slot, and later plugin-loading failures cannot skip drain. It is stack-local and never copied/moved. No new raw handle/manual cleanup is introduced.
4. DEX cleanup and external stop serialize through stop_mu_. Watchdog is armed BEFORE mutex acquisition. Existing main cleanup does not hold Guardian's mutex when the later DEX guard takes stop_mu_, so no new reverse lock pair. OS observers' stop is sequentially repeatable but not concurrent-entry-safe; serialization preserves that distinction.
5. DEX observation callback captures this but only borrows cfg_, dex_seq_, refusal counter and stream mutex/holder. It does not access Guardian/Spark/plugin descriptors/pool. All these agent members outlive the new local guard, and the health-error callback owns its shared atomic. Thus DEX may drain after other run cleanup without borrowed-data escape. Windows late EventLog callback state retains the existing stopping/in-flight protection.
6. The additional ShutdownDeadlineGuard owns shared State; its bounded detached watchdog captures State plus stateless hard_exit action, never AgentImpl. Cancellation wakes it; allocation/spawn failure and expiry hard_exit4. The existing RAII abstraction has no new ownership ambiguity. Its independent deadline covers partial-start and stop_mu wait as well as observer joins.
7. main unpublishes and joins the POSIX watcher; Windows console/SCM synchronization excludes in-flight external stop before member destruction. F3 Guardian/Spark detached-worker accounting remains unchanged and is sampled after run cleanup. No signal-handler logging/locking is introduced.

Resource Ledger coverage: gate1.txt names modified three unique_ptr slots, DEX borrowed callback, local scopeguard, stop_mu lock_guard and watchdog sharedstate/thread; no omitted new resource boundary found.

Finding SG-STARTUP-TRUTH-1:
- Trigger: reading the SparkAvailability::Unwired arm after this lifecycle-publication change.
- Location: agents/core/src/agent.cpp near line1441 (case GuardianEngine::SparkAvailability::Unwired).
- Summary: it still says the arm is reachable because stop already ran during boot; external stop now cannot call Guardian::stop before wire_spark_engine/start_local finish. The named trigger no longer explains this arm. Keep a defensive arm but correct its explanation.
- IMPACT [I9]; EXPOSURE [E3,E6]; epistemic_status likely; native LOW; derived INFO/NICE; policy_floor none; classification truth-contradiction.
- No operational error observed; no new runtime blocker asserted. This is domain truth, not wording. C++/docs owner should reconcile it, not inflate independent discovery from this handoff.

Validation limits: no runtime tests executed by this reviewer. Root's original TSan failure is retained, not treated as fixed solely by this proof. Require focused early-SIGTERM regression and affected supported-platform validation before closing the original runtime finding.

Decision: source review PASS; runtime/sanitizer closure PENDING. No new ownership or lifetime finding. Resource Ledger: complete. This is a separate role pass by the same reviewer who performed Gate2 security; it is not an independent cold discovery and does not increase reporter counts.

Gate2 known NICE Unwired explanation, docs fragment/reference corrections belong to their existing findings, not new cpp-safety rows.

Checks:
- Slot writes: guardian_ assigned once during run, DEX assigned once beforestart; Spark assigned then reset only on boot degradation. Release publication follows all these mutations and Guardian start_local; acquire in externalstop strictly precedes each pointer read. No reset/reseat after publication was found. Thread creation publishes subsequent readers; this fix does not add an unsynchronized second publication path.
- ScopeExit stop_dex is local and never copied/moved. It captures only this while AgentImpl remains owned by main/service. Armed before factory/start, so null-acquisition and partialstart unwinds are safe. The guard is independent of lifecycle_boot_done_ and stop_completed_, preventing once-flag poisoning after an early external stop.
- DEX is joined/drained by run-thread guard, then sequentially stoppable again by destructor. Concurrent externalstop is excluded by stop_mu_. Both stop and new guard arm their own ShutdownDeadlineGuard before waiting for stop_mu_, retaining a bound even if another caller wedges.
- Lock ordering: externalstop owns stop_mu_ then briefly enters Guardian::stop; the run cleanup's Guardian::stop has returned and released its internal lock before the later DEX guard attempts stop_mu_. No inverse held-lock cycle is introduced. DEX callback never takes stop_mu_ or Guardian mutex and does not call Agent::stop. Windows in-flight drain, Linux thread join and macOS stop-wake/join remain valid.
- DEX callback dependencies outlive guard: cfg_, eventcounter, refusalcounter, stream_write_mu_, guardian_sink_stream_. No borrowed Guardian/Spark/plugin/dispatch-pool access exists in that callback; healthfailure owns a shared atomic. Therefore later ordering of DEX guard after existing engine/plugin cleanup is safe. The new guard also covers exceptions before existing run cleanup is armed; as with all RAII it operates on stack unwinding, not a promise to recover from terminate/hard_exit.
- Guardian-before-Spark stop and reverse member destruction order unchanged. The existing Guardian/Spark run cleanup deliberately has no publication gate. No worker can remain active merely because an early stop set stop_completed_.
- F3 sum unchanged: Guardian's physical IO worker count plus independently constructed shared spark_detached_workers_. No new dereference through Spark or the lifecycle latch was introduced. Failed/startup-degraded Spark still contributes physically alive detachedworkers via independentlyowned counter. Existing main/service orphan guard remains after run cleanup and before AgentImpl destruction.
- New watchdog uses existing noncopyable/nonmovable ShutdownDeadlineGuard. Shared State and bounded detached worker capture no AgentImpl, raw resource orborrowed stack. Cancellation/wake and last-reference release preserve ownership; constructor/spawnfailure hard_exit4. No owningraw boundary omitted from ledger.
- No new C ABI/context/cast, pointerview, shellstring, manual fclose/close/free or platformhandle transfer. Mechanical raw-API sweep of modified source shows unchanged existing sites; modified ownership sites are the three unique_ptrs, DEX callback/lifetime, local ScopeExit/lock and watchdog documented in ledger.

Required validation before overall closure: focused early-SIGTERM original harness (including P1i/P1iii) on TSan, plus full affected agent suite and normal-exit lifecycle repetitions; ASan/UBSan for callback/resource lifetime; Windows MSVC agent build/runtime for EventLog callback drain and macOS/AppleClang for kqueue stop/join path or explicit validated scope. ===== quality-engineer (verbatim) =====
Gate 3 / Gate 8: quality-engineer source review

Scope and authority
Reviewed the current agents/core/src/agent.cpp startup publication and early DEX cleanup change using .claude/agents/quality-engineer.md, the canonical shared-preamble.txt, gate1.txt, and the relevant fixture and isolation contract in docs/testing/unit-test-conventions.md. No source edits, remote runs, or candidate runtime claims were made. Root owns the ongoing regression development and execution.

Verdict
No new QA blocker identified by source review. Execution evidence is PENDING. Earlier integration results apply to the pre-fix source and cannot close this candidate. Baseline P1i and P1iii reportedly reproduced TSan exit 66 both in the full suite and focused runs; those retained results belong to root's evidence record, not to tests run by this reviewer.

Assertion review
The existing tests/shell/test_blocked_log_sink_sigterm.sh is a meaningful regression surface. P1i and P1iii send TERM after the startup log readiness marker, record the actual child wait status, require exit 0, and fail on an eight-second bound. An exit 66 is rejected. P1iii only checks the JSON log file after a confirmed clean exit, and dumps transcripts on failure. The harness returns nonzero when FAIL_COUNT is nonzero. Its separate fault scenarios permit only their enumerated expected statuses; they do not turn arbitrary crashes into success.

The readiness log does not force a particular publication interleaving. A successful run therefore establishes that observed execution, not all schedules. The reproduced baseline shows the harness can expose this race; it is not a false-green test. Repeated runs of the same baseline and candidate with the same TSan options strengthen regression evidence. A controlled boot-window case would add useful deterministic coverage without replacing the original scenario.

Fixture and cleanup review
The shell harness allocates a mktemp directory with the yuzu_test_ prefix, gives scenarios separate data, plugin, and log subdirectories, and tracks launched child processes for trap cleanup. The refused outbound peer 127.0.0.1:1 is not a fixed test listener shared between jobs. Optional plugin links target existing build artifacts read-only. Wait loops and backstop termination are bounded; remaining children are killed and the private directory is removed on exit. The Linux-only registration is intentional because the blocked-pipe observation uses /proc. Its coverage must not be presented as native Windows or Darwin signal integration coverage.

Existing relevant tests
- tests/unit/test_dex_signals.cpp: factory non-null and initially unarmed assertions. Native Linux and Darwin branches exercise observer start followed by stop; the Windows branch does not exercise that lifecycle here.
- tests/unit/test_shutdown_deadline_guard.cpp: cancellation before deadline, destructor cancellation, one-shot firing, independent guards, cancellation/deadline races, and default hard-exit behavior; noncopyable and nonmovable compile-time assertions exist.
- Existing DEX parsing and privacy tests remain relevant but do not observe AgentImpl pointer publication or the placement of its new local cleanup guard.
No new RAII type, parser, store, or source file was introduced, so no new type-trait assertion, parser fuzz target, CRUD suite, or Meson source entry is required by this fix alone.

Pending execution and coverage acceptance
1. Root's candidate focused P1i/P1iii runs, full sanitizer suite, and native Windows/Darwin validation must finish before claiming closure. Record exact source identity, options, statuses and sanitizer findings.
2. The intended controlled startup case should demonstrate TERM before lifecycle_boot_done_ publication, followed by bounded clean exit with no sanitizer report. A held Guardian boot lock can control that window if it is acquired by the test before launching the agent and the test verifies the agent reached the intended wait. Use unique state and always release the external lock in cleanup. Do not make an earlier readiness log alone stand in for proof of the controlled window.
3. The new early DEX guard specifically protects unwinding after observer start and before the later main cleanup guard is armed. If a disposable injection seam is used, assert the observer callback/thread is drained before owner destruction and that stop is not concurrent with external stop. A seam that throws only after the later cleanup guard is armed does not cover the newly protected interval. An uncaught exception in POSIX main is not guaranteed to unwind; do not claim that this guard repairs that separate pre-existing exception-policy limitation.
4. If no partial-start/unwind or repeated-stop case is added, explicitly record that narrow coverage limitation and rationale. Ordinary missing coverage is SHOULD, not a false-green policy floor. The current candidate has no such new test; this concrete absence is recorded below, independently of pending test executions.

Q1: OPEN — early DEX cleanup interval lacks direct regression coverage.
Location: agents/core/src/agent.cpp, stop_dex ScopeExit before make_dex_observer(); tests/unit/test_dex_signals.cpp factory lifecycle test; tests/shell/test_blocked_log_sink_sigterm.sh P1i/P1iii.
Trigger: an exception after DEX has started but before the later main cleanup guard is armed, or external stop overlapping the run-thread DEX drain.
Impact: I9, latent regression exposure; this review does not assert that the candidate has a leak, hang, or concurrent-stop defect. The missing observation leaves these newly protected ownership paths untested.
Exposure: E3, ordinary shutdown or startup failure after observer initialization; E5, scheduling affects concurrent teardown.
Epistemic status: likely, from inspected source and test coverage; no runtime defect was observed by this reviewer.
Severity native: SHOULD (ordinary missing ownership/partial-failure/concurrent-teardown coverage under the quality-engineer brief). Derived band: INFO from I9. Nonblocking; no policy floor. The ordinary missing-coverage classification remains SHOULD; it is not evidence of a runtime failure and not false-green closure.
Acceptance: add a regression seam that fails after DEX start and before the later cleanup guard, observes observer drain before owner destruction, and exercises repeated stop serialized with the external stop path. A test starting after the later cleanup guard does not cover this interval. Prefer a disposable test observer or external instrumentation over adding production behavior solely for testing. Alternatively, record an explicit reviewed deferral with rationale and bounded scope. Passing the pending full suites alone does not directly cover the absent injection path.

Runtime status remains pending. Q1 is independent of unfinished execution, and can be resolved by root's ongoing regression work.

Gate 4 happy-path — startup lifecycle fix
Read .claude/agents/happy-path.md, canonical shared preamble, Gate 1 and completed Gate 2/3 reports. This is a separate role, not another independent discovery of known findings.

Normal startup constructs/configures Guardian and DEX, initializes Spark or the deliberate disabled/degraded state, wires Guardian and starts local enforcement before release-publishing the three slots. A subsequent external stop acquires that publication, so it sees complete initialization. The immediate request flag and RPC cancellation still run before worker drains.
Normal shutdown preserves Guardian before Spark. External stop cancels the subscribe context before DEX drain, then stops Guardian, DEX and Spark while serializing duplicate callers. Run-thread cleanup independently stops Guardian and Spark, quiesces runtime workers and plugins, then the earlier DEX guard drains under stop_mu_. Repeated Guardian/Spark stops preserve their existing idempotency/completion contracts; repeated DEX stops are serialized and each platform implementation tolerates a completed prior stop. DEX callbacks do not borrow plugins or engine objects.
The one-way publication latch does not alter protocol results, registration, command dispatch, persistence, authentication or capability discovery. The owner lifetime barriers remain in main()/service_win.cpp. No normal successful path skips DEX cleanup merely because an earlier stop set stop_completed_.

Read unhappy-path brief and canonical shared preamble; applied Gate1 ownership model and completed Gate2/3 reports. Reviewed current candidate and relevant startup/stop/cleanup/main/SCM paths. No remote run or source mutation performed.

Boundaries: OS shutdown signal/SCM callback → AgentImpl atomics/slot publication → Guardian/Spark/DEX native workers → gRPC stream callback → run cleanup → owner destruction. No added external data parser, network admission, store mutation or authorization boundary.

| U09 | duplicate stop while first is slow | Each caller arms its watchdog before mutex wait; second cannot enter concurrent DEX stop. Completed latch only suppresses duplicate external work. | Overlap two callers and delayed observer. |
| U10 | run cleanup overlaps externalstop | Guardian mutex is released before later DEX guard takes stop_mu; no reverse held-lock pair. DEX stop is sequential, not assumed concurrent-safe. | Q1 overlapping drain oracle. |

Shutdown remains per endpoint; hard_exit4 is deliberately nonzero and preserves supervisor visibility. Silent lost-stop risk is addressed by the retained stop_requested_ flag and ungated run cleanup; sanitizer success alone must not replace actual process-exit assertions.

No new findings to derive severity for. Known QA Q1 is a coverage limitation, not a demonstrated candidate defect; no false-green closure is asserted. This source pass does not close the original verified TSan finding or authorize commit before root's execution results and final evidence review.


===== consistency-auditor (verbatim) =====
Gate 4 / Gate 8: consistency-auditor

Reviewed source identity
HEAD: 052d9039d8dd360ff4cc135ec8caa6fb217f547c (working candidate is uncommitted).
agent.cpp Git blob: bd21dcf23c26eaa13026459cad4b4a949db5423f
agent.cpp SHA-256: adb516a51671bfe8762de9247b5bc4b08b70b3c0689bb3bf4929b3998bf4069d
Authority: .claude/agents/consistency-auditor.md, canonical shared-preamble.txt and Gate 1 resource ledger. Inspected the actual working diff and related lifecycle entrypoints, observer teardown and detached-worker accounting. No code edits or runtime tests performed.

Verdict
No additional independent consistency finding. Existing D1/D2/D3 documentation findings remain applicable to this inspected snapshot and are not recounted here. Candidate runtime validation and Q1 coverage work remain pending; this source review is not a runtime pass.

State and temporal consistency
The release store follows Guardian construction, DEX start, Spark construction/degradation, wiring and Guardian start_local. The acquire load in external stop encloses all three unique_ptr reads. Assignment/reset searches show their mutations occur before publication; subsequent member destruction follows the external-caller lifetime barrier. No alternate occurrence of the old latch remains in executable agent.cpp code.

A stop before publication sets cancellation and stop_completed_ while skipping the workers. The run-thread cleanup deliberately does not consult either the publication latch or stop_completed_. Consequently, the first external stop cannot poison the run-thread cleanup by marking skipped work complete. Guardian/Spark retain their own downstream repeat-call safety; DEX is serialized with external stop using stop_mu_. The new DEX guard is armed before factory/start and destroys later than the existing main cleanup guard, so it also covers a stack unwind before the latter is constructed.

The different cleanup orders are intentional: external stop cancels the stream and stops Guardian, DEX, then Spark; the run-thread guards stop Guardian and Spark, release plugin/pool resources, then drain DEX. The DEX signal callback borrows AgentImpl members for event construction and stream delivery, not Guardian/Spark/plugin/pool objects. Its error callback owns a shared health atomic. Those member lifetimes outlast both local guards. There is no new cross-component ownership transfer.

Each cleanup has its own ShutdownDeadlineGuard; the new DEX guard starts before waiting for stop_mu_. This gives that cleanup a deadline even if an external stop holds the mutex. It does not promise a single combined twenty-second bound for all sequential shutdown phases, nor make underlying joins intrinsically timed.

Platform consistency
Linux DEX stops its poll thread before clearing callbacks. Darwin wakes kqueue, joins and cleans up, including partial start. Windows joins its poller, clears subscriptions outside the callback-state mutex, waits for in-flight callbacks, then clears sinks. Their sequential repeated stop is compatible with run cleanup followed by later member destruction; external stop cannot concurrently enter the new DEX guard's stop body. The existing Windows console/SCM g_agent_mu barrier and POSIX watcher join still protect AgentImpl destruction. No new platform-specific syscall or signal-handler work was added.

The pre-existing POSIX main uncaught-exception limitation remains: a bare agent->run() call has no enclosing catch, so stack unwinding on an escaping exception is not guaranteed before terminate. Windows service has an outer catch. The new guard provides cleanup on actual unwind; it does not establish exception-policy parity. This is the already identified R-EXCEPTION context, not a new finding caused by this fix.

Detached-worker accounting
The F3 sum remains GuardianEngine::active_io_workers plus the independently owned spark_detached_workers_ atomic. It does not dereference SparkEngine or depend on successful publication/wiring. Counter ownership therefore continues through Spark boot failure/reset and detached completion. Main/service sampling remains after run exit and the relevant stop/lifetime barrier; the new lifecycle latch is not substituted for this separate accounting contract.

Documentation consistency and existing findings
D1: inspected fragment still lacks the startup-shutdown behavior bullet.
D2: docs/yuzu-guardian-design-v1.1.md:2681, spark_detached_call.hpp:44 and test_spark_detached_call.cpp:768 still name spark_boot_done_. Their independent counter semantics remain correct; the identifier is stale.
D3: the Unwired branch comment/log in agent.cpp still attributes that branch to an external stop during boot. The publication gate now prevents that early external Guardian stop. This is the already recorded truth nit; no new independent count.
Root/parent are updating these references. Recheck their final content and source identity after edits rather than treating this snapshot as the completed documentation review.

No API, wire schema, persistence, authorization, classifier, CI cache or transport status mapping changed in this fix. No new ledger finding proposed.

Gate 6 / Gate 8: Enterprise Readiness Review

Reviewed scope and authority
Current startup lifecycle fix in agents/core/src/agent.cpp, Git blob bd21dcf23c26eaa13026459cad4b4a949db5423f; current working diff remains this one runtime file. Prior Gate 2/3/4 findings are contextual and not independent discoveries. No remote or runtime tests were run.

Documentation impact
The operator-visible reliability change needs a release fragment describing safe shutdown during startup. This is existing D1, not a new finding. Existing D2 stale latch references and D3 Unwired explanation remain in the inspected snapshot and are owned by the earlier review. The fix introduces no CLI flag, environment variable, configuration default, API shape, permissions change, upgrade step or migration. It does not require a Breaking fragment or a new server-admin upgrade procedure. No customer should need to disable DEX or Spark to receive the fix.

Assurance impact
This change improves agent lifecycle reliability by publishing initialized workers before external stop can access their pointers and by adding a separately bounded DEX cleanup path. It does not change the DEX collection catalog, data retention, audit semantics, authentication, transport encryption or the shared-responsibility boundary. Workstream G's DPA, questionnaire and architecture disclosures therefore need no new data-processing or control claim for this fix alone.

Assurance evidence must distinguish source review from actual testing. Candidate full sanitizer, focused regression and native platform results are pending. Pre-fix passing suites cannot substantiate the new source. Existing Q1 identifies a narrow partial-failure/concurrent-stop coverage gap; it is not a demonstrated runtime defect. Do not claim all exception paths unwind safely: the pre-existing POSIX uncaught-exception limitation is outside what the new ScopeExit establishes. Separate cleanup deadlines also do not establish a single total twenty-second shutdown SLA.

Deployment impact
No installer, service registration, port, credential, privilege or deployment topology changes. The same executable rollout mechanism applies on Linux, Windows and Darwin. An ordinary stop during initialization now skips unpublished worker pointers; the run thread remains responsible for draining them. Native service/console/signal validation should accompany release evidence because the lifetime barriers differ across platforms. Neither a schema migration nor special rollback sequence is introduced.

Integration impact
No new external network flow, SIEM event, metrics contract, IdP integration, REST/MCP capability or discovery entry is introduced. The fix does not change A5 metadata or its residuals.

# TLS integration fixture diagnosis — PR7659

Status: TLS fixture repair validated locally. This is incremental test-fixture evidence, not a whole-PR governance verdict or permission to merge.

The unchanged integration snapshot078c8537b065e66e3e7973f2eff8a0b2234e5e81891c3b820aadbadbb7623c1e reproduced the retained failure on2026-11-14:46/47 integration checks,30/31CommonTest cases. In the November13 retained run, rpc_notify_stream_status saw unknown_session delta1 when expecting0. In the new baseline it timed out waiting for exactly1 after the deliberate forged request while an earlier fixture's invalid notices kept retrying.

Root cause: agent_disconnect_notification created real-disconnect-agent with fabricated real-disc-sess instead of registering with the server. The server correctly refused it. Gateway NOTIFY-ACK retry state survived the test, contaminating the later process-wide desynchronisation metric and generic notify_stream telemetry. This is test interference, not evidence of TLS handshake or OpenSSL corruption. Deliberately refused notices can legitimately be retried, so exactly-once counting was an invalid fixture assumption.

Treatment changes only gateway/apps/yuzu_gw/integration_test/yuzu_gw_real_upstream_SUITE.erl and one existing changelog fragment. Register a real session; await CONNECTED and DISCONNECTED RPC completion; assert zero unknown-session desynchronisation and verify the server removes the session. Explicit per-key notify_settled barriers prevent intentional forged-notice retries leaking beyond the RPC fixture. Keep exact-zero valid-session assertion; require at least one rejection for the invalid session. Keep RPC-latency success checks because settlement alone can also mean a lost notice.

Resource ownership: telemetry attachment detached in finally; spawned stream process killed in finally; unlinked owner stopped with shutdown in inner finally; reply aliases unaliased in finally. Review found the initially used normal stop skips owner's cleanup on early assertion failures; corrected to shutdown, which follows shutdown_cleanup/do_cleanup. No new production hooks, API, crypto policy, suppression or timeout extension.

Final source snapshot52ef44c0301bb1a06aab2a21af59e067ba9b534743da41ceb0340e2d37120f55 contains6377files and differs from078 only in the suite/changelog. Final suiteSHA9c95af05fcde3699c08157aa13dd5a9b7323f4ab07a1702af4bc1173c8f174b1. Earlier broad Linux185/185TSan, final agent22/22TSan, agent/docs148/148ASan and Windows33/33 results apply to their separately recorded prior source identities; production code is unchanged by this increment.

- Original full-stack baseline: exit1,46/47integration;30/31CT. Failure retained.
- Regression control restoring only fake session assignment: exit1 at disconnect fixture's exact-zero assertion (got1);30otherCTcases passed. The stronger test detects the source of contamination deterministically.
- First corrected full TLS stack: integration.exit0,47/47checks and31/31CT. External runner exited2 after the successful integration due to being replaced while running; orchestration-note.md preserves the distinction. Followupsv1 exited1, no product failure hidden.
- Exception control with old normal stop: exit1, server-session cleanup timed out;30otherCTcases passed. Disposable suiteSHAf9d1471b90630b8b8dc3b5837366970f7322101bda94b37e26e524ce6e61e475.

- Production Dialyzer analyzed52modules and exited0. CT compile also passed for the changed integration suite.
- Remote6377file final-source verificationPASS. Changelog synchronized after runtime tests; executable/test source unchanged. All60archive member hashes verified locally; archiveSHAb74e1b2326e381819aaf53c40a6786dc4ca21b0a6e177896a3306729420eedff.

Title: Test the agent's early DEX unwind drain and serialized repeated stop

Proposed labels: `task`, `P2`, `needs-triage`, `reliability`

## Context

PR #7659 fixes the agent startup/shutdown publication race tracked by #2181. It also arms a DEX cleanup guard before observer construction/start, so actual stack unwinding before the later run cleanup still drains the observer under a deadline. Existing startup-SIGTERM tests reproduce the original race and reject TSan exit 66, but do not directly observe this earlier exception path while the agent object remains alive.

Add a narrow regression for that cleanup interval and its serialization with external stop. This is a coverage task, not a report of an observed defect in the new guard. It does not ask for a new public agent API or production fault-injection switch.

## Evidence

Line references below were checked against `origin/dev` at `4277cc2702318095a7e17fdb5ff5f5bc4882c6ae`; the pending PR adds the guard and shifts lines.

- `agents/core/src/agent.cpp:985` creates the DEX observer; `:1003` starts it. The later general cleanup guard is armed at `:1152`, leaving a distinct interval for the new early guard to protect.
- `agents/core/src/agent.cpp:4040` serializes external `stop()` under `stop_mu_`. The new early DEX guard uses that same mutex because native observer stops are not concurrent-entry safe.
- `tests/unit/test_dex_signals.cpp:1405` checks the factory, and its Linux/macOS branches exercise ordinary observer start/stop. It does not invoke the AgentImpl unwind path.
- `tests/shell/test_blocked_log_sink_sigterm.sh` cases P1i/P1iii assert bounded clean process exits after startup TERM and provide the race regression. Process exit alone cannot show that a partial-start observer drained before owner destruction.
- Governance finding `startup-qa-001` in `governance.d/7659-dev-integration-bd6b62.jsonl`: native SHOULD, derived INFO/NICE, no policy floor; the untested interval is acknowledged rather than certified by unrelated green tests.

## Acceptance criteria

- Force a failure after the DEX observer has started and before the later general run cleanup guard is armed, using a test-owned observer or equivalent isolated instrumentation.
- Catch the failure in the test while keeping the agent object alive; assert the observer's callback/thread has drained before owner destruction. A destructor-only drain or a throw after the later guard is armed must not satisfy the test.
- Exercise repeated stop with an external stop caller and assert observer stop does not enter concurrently. Keep the observation bounded and independent of sleeps used as the sole synchronization oracle.
- Demonstrate that removing/moving the early guard or removing its serialization makes the corresponding assertion fail.
- Preserve the separate POSIX uncaught-exception limitation: this test proves cleanup during actual unwinding, not that POSIX main catches every escaping exception.
- Use unique test resources and the repository's canonical test registration/build conventions; do not add a production fault-injection option just for this test.

## Origin

PR #7659 startup-fix Gate 3/8 review, finding `startup-qa-001`; reviewed deferral approved by root on 2026-11-13. Draft only: no issue has been created and no ledger disposition is invented.

Read-only GitHub connector dedupe searches on 2026-11-13, all `repo:DevNullLtd/Yuzu is:issue is:open`:

- `"dex_observer"`: #1474, #4925. Neither covers early-unwind test coverage.
- `"DEX" "cleanup"`: #5812, #5102, #2181, #4687, #4458, #4462, #1847, #320, #7372, #6718, #5687. #2181 is the runtime race this PR fixes; its acceptance asks for boot-window TSan regression, not this partial-start observer oracle.
- `"observer" "unwind"`: #3338. This covers agent stop completion/hard-exit handler parity, a different production change.
- `"DEX" "partial"`: #7537, #7519, #7548, #7531, #6551, #1357, #2260, #7147, #2556, #1554, #1605, #4978, #4552. None addresses the named early guard regression.
- `"stop_dex"`: no results.

Related: #2181, #3338, #4925. Recheck dedupe before filing if the draft is used later. Follow the canonical governance post-run sequence; record the resulting issue number by an append-only ledger row, without marking this missing test fixed.

===== END VERBATIM startup-fix-review/qa-q1-followup-draft.md =====

PR #7659 fixes the agent startup/shutdown publication race tracked by #2181. It also arms a DEX cleanup guard before observer construction/start, so actual stack unwinding before the later run cleanup still drains the observer under a deadline. Source disposition for tls-fixture-cleanup-001: treatment addresses the identified wrong terminate branch. This is independent source re-review, not runtime verification. At suite:336 gen_statem:stop now requests shutdown. Owner terminate at yuzu_gw_agent.erl:789 invokes shutdown_cleanup; connected-state fallback at825 invokes do_cleanup at962. That removes the owner's local registration through the fenced deregistration call, fails pending requests and calls announce_disconnected, which casts the upstream DISCONNECTED notice using the original session/home identity. Normal termination at784 would only owner_delete and is no longer selected by this explicit cleanup call.

Important limit: gen_statem:stop waits for owner termination, not the remote server's acknowledgement of the cast. Neither this treatment nor the existing catch guarantees remote cleanup if upstream is unavailable, or if the bounded stop times out. The correct source claim is that the live owner's shutdown cleanup is invoked and dispatches the notification. Bounded failure of cleanup remains visible only through the already-failed test; this change does not promise acknowledged cleanup under dependency failure. No additional concrete finding.
Consistency: no new finding. Existing AgentId, SessionId and owner StreamHomeId remain authoritative; treatment selects the intended lifecycle branch without changing key shape, protocol or production state machine.

TLS incremental fixture correction: Gate 4
Date: 2026-11-14
Scope: incremental-fixture-fix.diff only; startup changes excluded. Happy-path, unhappy-path and consistency-auditor briefs read; canonical severity and prose contract applied. Source-only review, no runtime execution, no whole-PR approval.

Happy-path verdict: no finding under functioning dependencies. Server-minted session replaces fabricated disconnect session; CONNECTED RPC latency and server known-state assertions precede stream termination. The DISCONNECTED RPC, registry removal and server unknown-state checks establish observable cleanup. The direct notify case sends valid then forged session IDs with stable per-key home IDs, waits for settlement after each, and checks zero then >=1 desync. Allowing >=1 reflects documented retries instead of suppressing a real refusal. Settlement alone does not prove success: separate RPC telemetry plus server metric assertions remain required and are present. The test does not prove absence of every unrelated concurrent producer; its supported evidence scope is the dedicated sequential fixture, not a production traffic theorem.

Unhappy-path verdict: one LOW/NICE finding, tls-fixture-cleanup-001 (below). Missing metrics fail in strict integration mode; non-strict unreadable metrics are an existing limitation, not new evidence suitable for closure. Lost settlement reply or stopped upstream times out and fails; unalias executes even on failure. Delayed replies are dropped through the revoked alias. Each cleanup wait is bounded; telemetry detaches in an inner after even if cleanup fails. If the first cleanup wait itself times out, the second is not attempted: this remains a red test rather than false-green evidence. No new production protocol is introduced. No new speculative production risks filed.

Consistency-auditor verdict: no independent finding. SessionId is the server-minted value for the owner and both server-state probes. notify_settled uses exactly the AgentId/SessionId/Home keys submitted by the same sender, matching production queue identity. Owner do_cleanup uses its original StreamHomeId; the ordinary disconnect test does not substitute a new one. Transport-success telemetry is not mistaken for application acceptance: unknown-session delta distinguishes deliberate refusal. No wire schema, auth configuration, REST/MCP classifier or deployment contract changes. Existing Gate 2 wording assessment remains valid; cleanup finding is implementation truth, not a duplicate wording issue.

Risk register / finding:
ID: tls-fixture-cleanup-001
Owner: unhappy-path (gateway fixture exception cleanup)
Location: gateway/apps/yuzu_gw/integration_test/yuzu_gw_real_upstream_SUITE.erl:336
Trigger: a new disconnect-case assertion fails while AgentPid is still connected, before exit(StreamHandler, kill), for example the first zero-desync assertion fails due to unexpected background traffic.
Observed from source: after invokes gen_statem:stop(AgentPid, normal,5000). yuzu_gw_agent:terminate(normal,...) at784 only owner_delete; it explicitly assumes the disconnected-state entry already cleaned up. This failure path has not entered disconnected. The outer stream kill occurs after the owner is gone. The end_per_testcase registry deregistration removes local state but does not send the missing upstream DISCONNECTED. A real server session/placement can therefore remain after the failed fixture rather than being ended by its owner.
Expected: exception cleanup invokes the owner's shutdown cleanup while it is still alive.
Mitigation: use gen_statem:stop(AgentPid, shutdown,5000); terminate(shutdown,...) calls shutdown_cleanup, whose connected-state branch calls do_cleanup at825, deregisters the session and announces DISCONNECTED at962-973. Already-gone owner remains harmless under catch.
Impact: I8 (failed-fixture residual state/noise; no claimed production corruption or control failure).
Exposure: E5 (partial test failure), E6 (new call site is integration-test-only; this wrong cleanup call cannot run in shipped production).
Epistemic status: likely, reasoned from owner code actually read, not fault-injection observed.
Derived band: LOW. severity_native: low. Governance tier: NICE. No policy floor applies; no false-green closure assertion is being offered for this exception path.
Blast radius: this real-upstream test rig after an already-failed testcase, until later teardown/expiry. No production-scope expansion.
Independent concurrence: final_safety_consistency agreed after the path was raised; it will not file a duplicate independent finding.
Gate 5 handoff: one bounded scenario worth validating if addressed—fail an assertion before stream kill and confirm owner shutdown enters do_cleanup. No synthetic production chaos is warranted by this fixture-only diff.

Cross-review addendum, source still cce52e707d / a0ccab6711.
Gate4 reviewer found LOW test-only exception cleanup issue; Gate3 peer concurred after tracing production termination. I independently read yuzu_gw_agent.erl:784-795: terminate(normal) deletes owner bookkeeping only; terminate(shutdown) calls shutdown_cleanup, which routes connected state to do_cleanup. Thus a failure before the explicit stream kill followed by normal stop leaves the server session live; subsequent kill has no owner to notify. Corrective recommendation: change finally stop reason to shutdown. Parent notified; no edit made here.
This qualifies earlier Gate1/SRE statements: process and telemetry resources are released, but application session cleanup on early assertion failure is incomplete. Security production-surface verdict unchanged. Gate3/4 own the finding (no independent-count inflation); Gate5 design now required because Gate4 raised a risk. Final aggregate review pending treatment and runtime.

(2) The Forensics plugin set is DERIVED from the capability catalogue (`forensics_plugin_set.hpp`), never hand-kept; an empty set refuses boot. (3) UNKNOWN fails closed: `resolve_forensics_visible` is tri-state; an unresolved decision is a retryable 503 / MCP `kInternalError` / HTML notice (counted), never a silent partial 200, for every caller class; it decides via `AuthRoutes::probe_permission_unavailable_aware` — NEVER plain `probe_permission` and NEVER `require_fleet_read`/`authorize_list_read` (they admit every caller while RBAC is off); a service-scoped token is default-denied (definitive 403). (4) The server's own readers (policy evaluator, result-set materializer, bundle collation) use the tier filter, never an unrestricted read; the live output bus emits nothing for a Forensics frame; a Forensics plugin's failure TEXT reaches every sink (tracker `error_detail`, outbox/bus, failure notification, analytics, webhook/offload) ONLY through `sanitize_forensics_error_detail` via `AgentServiceImpl::failure_text_for_sinks` — a NEW sink must use it; bundle collation serving a Forensics step audits `response.forensics.read` `surface=bundle`. (5) `ForensicsAnalyst` (four read pairs, fleet-wide, system role) never gains Execute or a write; `server.cpp` must keep wiring the plugin set and resolver into every surface (`tests/test_forensics_tier_wiring_lexical.py`). (6) NO scoped (management-group) `Forensics:Read` read tier (group-scope assignment accepts `Operator`/`Viewer` only) — adding one needs a production authoring path, fresh review and end-to-end tests. (7) PROVENANCE is server-authored: the plugin label is `command_execution.plugin`, read by the ONE function `resolve_response_provenance` (`response_provenance.hpp`) per frame per ingest path, NEVER parsed from the echoed command id (a per-target fan-out loop writes its records through a per-loop `DispatchRecordLatch`); a frame with no usable record is `~unverified`, a Forensics-set member. (8) DEPLOYMENT CONTRACT (hard cutover; owner scope decision 2026-10-24, not an acceptance of any residual): fresh database only, every writer on the new build, earlier binaries excluded; procedural, not enforced — no doc may describe an upgrade path, mixed-build operation, a return to an earlier build or retained earlier-build copies as supported. Full text: the three docs in the Doc column (`docs/authz-model.md` "Reading the stored rows", the ladder's "The Forensics tier on stored-response reads", upgrading "Deployment contract").
  Neither changed path nor its incremental behavior touches this named surface/trigger.
.claude/routed-concerns-software-estate.md:23 NO MATCH **`certificate_inventory` daily-sync source and `CertificateInventoryStore` (schema `certificate_inventory_store`).** CATASTROPHIC-IF-VIOLATED: (1) the agent writer (it lands with the agent collector) is the CANONICAL `cert` record contract and `certificate_inventory_ingestion.{hpp,cpp}` follows it field-for-field; change the writer, the server parser and BOTH golden tests (the writer's and `test_certificate_inventory.cpp`) together, byte-identically. (2) Ingest (full and hash-skip), `delete_agent` and any retention prune take the SAME per-agent advisory lock, keyed `kCertInventoryAgentLockPrefix` (a stable constant, never derived from the schema name); ingest only TRIES it and answers `deferred`/`agent_busy` rather than parking a pooled connection. (3) Times are the plain DB clock; the ok-store sweep compares `full_report_at` for DIFFERENCE, never ordering. (4) A refused record is never truncated; an unknown (`?`) or not-applicable (empty) typed field is NULL, never a default. (5) A whole report that would be refused identically (size, version, header, caps) is a TERMINAL ack (`rejected`, no need_full), as is an ingest-budget overrun (`error/budget_exceeded`): both FREEZE the device and are why `YuzuCertInventoryReportsRejected` (increase over 1d, no hold) exists. A hash-only claim that is not 64 lowercase hex is answered need_full (`bad_claim`) and never reaches the store; MD5 and the path key stay in SQL (FIPS). (6) The unchanged-fact guard includes the child digest, so a SAN/EKU/CDP-only change is never skipped. (7) `sample_key` (agent_state, copied onto cert_fact) is server-assigned from the CSPRNG when the agent row is first inserted, never taken from the wire, never in a read/export/audit column, and never changed in place (so it is deliberately NOT in the unchanged-fact guard); a CSPRNG failure fails the ingest, there is no clock fallback.
  Neither changed path nor its incremental behavior touches this named surface/trigger.
.claude/routed-concerns-software-estate.md:24 NO MATCH **Software title normalisation layer** — `SoftwareTitleDimension` (the ADR-0012 §3 cross-store owner), the hourly `SoftwareNormalizationJob`, the Postgres-persisted alias pack and the permanent `title_dimension` / `curation_queue`. CATASTROPHIC-IF-VIOLATED: (1) a registry write that can change a title's resolution sets the affected `title_dimension.pack_version = ''` in the SAME transaction and MUST first take the blocking `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('software_normalization', 0))` that every pass tries, or a concurrent pass stamps the current version over the sentinel and the invalidation is lost (`ProductRegistryStore::upsert_product` and `upsert_alias` are such writers and do this themselves, the alias write marking every row stale; a new writer must too); (2) `title_dimension` is permanent and unbounded by design, so a prune is a separate clock-guarded-retention decision, never a quiet addition; (3) the queue reconcile is a derived-set reconcile with deliberately no would-wipe guard, and `rejected` rows are never deleted; (4) a title is LABELLED, never guessed: auto-birth stays off (`kAutoBirthEnabled`, behind a `static_assert`), the matcher's fuzzy tiers are never used, and an alias binds exactly one edition; (5) the pack parser is the trust boundary for every pack body (byte, row and field caps, NUL, reserved cpe tokens, no two rows for one product disagreeing).
  Neither changed path nor its incremental behavior touches this named surface/trigger.

Exact suite SHA256: 9c95af05fcde3699c08157aa13dd5a9b7323f4ab07a1702af4bc1173c8f174b1.

Thus this re-review is exactly one word; prior routing, severity preamble and role/reference reads apply.

Gateway-erlang: SOURCE PASS, zero remaining source findings in this incremental scope. terminate(shutdown, State, Data) invokes shutdown_cleanup then owner_delete. For the connected owner, shutdown_cleanup reaches do_cleanup, which marks ending, leaves the group, deregisters the fenced owner/session, fails pending work and calls announce_disconnected with the retained session/home. This fixes the previously skipped exception-path cleanup. Already disconnected/registering cases have explicit no-op branches; an already dead owner remains tolerated by the existing catch. Owner unlink occurs before this stop, so shutdown does not cascade to the testcase.
