TLS fixture incremental Gate 8: Gate 3 domain review

Scope: incremental-fixture-fix.diff only. No production source changes. Canonical shared severity block read verbatim; gateway-erlang and quality-engineer briefs and docs/erlang-gateway-build.md loaded. Two role passes by one reviewer, not two independent reporters.

Gateway-erlang verdict: SOURCE PASS, zero findings.
Quality-engineer verdict: SOURCE PASS, zero findings.
Runtime verdict: PENDING parent-owned RED/GREEN, compile, EUnit/CT and mandatory warning-free dialyzer. This reviewer ran no Erlang commands and claims no runtime pass.

Identity
gateway/apps/yuzu_gw/integration_test/yuzu_gw_real_upstream_SUITE.erl: cce52e707dc78a605858966b25e0f38489dc17165632d873263edfc9cb9cee28
changelog.d/20261110-nightly-shutdown-rotation.fixed.md: a0ccab67112b2fcdebcabef03842df7db970bbb2c703c26e7680cbca0b397842

Distinctive reviewed line: ?assertEqual(unknown, server_session_state(SessionId)),

Gateway-erlang reasoning
The disconnect fixture now obtains SessionId from real proxy_register and passes that exact ID into the real agent owner. TestPid is captured before spawn, correcting the prior self() evaluation inside the child. The stream process is unlinked; the owner is unlinked immediately after successful start_link, before deliberate stream kill or synchronous owner cleanup. Nested after clauses stop the owner on assertion failure, kill the stream and detach the unique telemetry handler. No production supervision or wire contract changes.

The settlement helper uses the existing public notify_settled/4 contract. Reviewed upstream clauses at notify_stream_status/5, notify_settled/4, handle_cast({notify_settled,...}) and release_settled/1. Both casts come from the testcase process to the same upstream process, so the barrier cannot overtake its notice. The exact key comprises agent ID, session ID and home ID; both generated Home values are retained and reused correctly. release_settled checks no held notification and no send in flight, then cancels the refused retry entry before acknowledging. A queued/in-flight resend cannot survive the completed barrier. It does not guarantee delivery: a counted-lost notice also settles, so the separate wire/metric assertions remain necessary.

alias([reply]) is supported by the pinned OTP. The alias is always unaliased in after, on acknowledgment, timeout or exception; late replies to a deactivated alias are dropped. Timeout calls ct:fail, not success. The outer after repeats settlement for both keys and always detaches telemetry even when settlement fails. Repeating settlement for an absent/finished key is explicitly supported. Cleanup failure remains a test failure; it is not converted to a pass. The owner stop catch tolerates the normal already-dead owner, while outer stream cleanup still executes.

Quality-engineer reasoning
Disconnect oracle is materially stronger than old registry-count/process-alive checks: it proves the minted session is known on the real server before stream death, verifies the exact local owner/session mapping, triggers the real DOWN cleanup, verifies local absence and probes that same session as unknown through the real BatchHeartbeat client. server_session_state fails on transport errors and ambiguous replies. An unrelated generic notify_stream latency event cannot by itself manufacture the required same-session known-to-unknown transition. Agent do_cleanup deregisters the exact owner/session and announce_disconnected carries its retained stream_home_id; no synthetic DISCONNECTED is substituted by the test.

For the intentionally unminted session, the server NotifyStreamStatus handler increments the op/outcome unknown_session metric and returns a successful gRPC response with acknowledged=false. Thus rpc_latency is transport-success evidence, not application acceptance. The >=1 assertion correctly permits legitimate refused retries; exact-one was not a valid contract. The valid notice first requires zero unknown-session increments. Both keys settle before metric inspection/exit, preventing this fixture's retry traffic from reaching the next case.

Telemetry remains generic by RPC name and the metric is aggregate. This is not newly introduced per-request tracing and is not proof of arbitrary concurrent-traffic isolation. In this sequential real-upstream fixture, the minted disconnect oracle plus key settlement and negative-case metric are sufficient for the claimed regression; no false-green closure finding identified. Parent should retain full ordered-suite GREEN and before-fix RED evidence, not infer them from this static review. An unrelated concurrent client using the same server could invalidate aggregate-metric attribution; the integration rig must remain isolated as already required.

No sleeps were added; the changed fixed sleep was replaced with observable events and server state. No new module or source-list wiring is needed. The changelog accurately describes fixture isolation, not a production TLS/OpenSSL behavior fix. No coverage-threshold or production-control claim is added.

Additional architect pass: SOURCE PASS, zero architecture findings. Loaded architect brief and relevant observability conventions (Prometheus prefix/labels, bounded counter semantics, telemetry versus application outcome). This fixture reads existing yuzu_server_gateway_route_desync_total op/outcome series and attaches to existing rpc_latency/rpc_error events. It changes neither descriptors, labels/cardinality, HELP, histogram buckets, event payloads, audit envelope nor alerts. notify_settled is an existing public owner-recovery API, not private gen_server-state introspection or a new production test hook. No new module, dependency, protobuf, ABI, storage or architectural invariant is introduced. Retry-aware >=1 tests the existing counter semantics rather than redefining them. Runtime remains pending.

Peer review correction — overrides the earlier zero-findings/cleanup-completeness assertion for gateway-erlang and quality-engineer:
final_docs_truth identified the normal-stop exception-cleanup defect; independently traced yuzu_gw_agent.erl:784-815 and concur. If an assertion fails while the owner remains connected, gen_statem:stop(AgentPid, normal,5000) invokes terminate(normal), which only deletes owner metadata and assumes disconnected entry already ran do_cleanup. It did not run in this path. Killing the stream after that owner has exited cannot notify it, leaving server session cleanup undone. Existing teardown's local deregistration is insufficient. Use shutdown reason to invoke shutdown_cleanup and the real disconnected notification. This is the peer-owned finding, not an independent reporter count. Architect verdict unaffected; source gate closure must use the peer disposition and corrected-source re-review. No runtime claim.
